are crossingless annular matchings, then the intersection pairing with
d_{o}
defines a map

\cdot d_{o}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\pi_{2}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{C}),\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0})\cup\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{1}))\to\mathbb{Z},\qquad u\mapsto u\cdot d_{o}.

Now, the symmetric product of the map
\pi_{A}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muA_{\tau_{o}}\to\mathbb{C}_{z}
gives us a map
\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(A_{\tau_{o}})\to\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{C})
.
Precomposing this map with the Hilbert-Chow morphism and restricting to
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
defines a map
\pi_{\Delta}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}\to\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{C})
. It is clear from the definitions that
\pi_{\Delta}(L_{\underline{c}_{i}})=\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{i})
for
i=0,1
, and we may choose our Hamiltonian perturbation
H
to be compatible with
\pi_{\Delta}
so that this remains true after perturbation.

Definition 3.4
.

For
x_{0},x_{1}\in\phi_{H}(\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}})\pitchfork\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}}
,
we define the relative winding grading
w(x_{0},x_{1})
to be
2(u\cdot d_{o})
where
u\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{C})

is a topological strip such that
\lim_{s\to-\infty}u(s,t)=\pi_{\Delta}(x_{0})
and
\lim_{s\to\infty}u(s,t)=\pi_{\Delta}(x_{1})
, and furthermore
u(s,0)\in\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0})
and
u(s,1)\in\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{1})
.

Here the factor of two is chosen in order to match the conventions of combinatorial annular Khovanov homology. The fact that the relative winding grading is independent of the choice of the topological strip
u
follows from the observation that
\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{C})=\mathbb{C}^{n}
,
\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0})
and
\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{1})
are all contractible. In Section
3.3
we discuss how to promote this relative grading to an absolute grading on the annular symplectic Khovanov homology.

Continuing with Floer theory, let
\mathcal{J}
be the space of
\omega
-tamed families of almost complex structures which agree with the complex structure of
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
near infinity, near the intersection
D_{HC}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
, and near the annular divisor
D_{o}
.
For a generic choice of
J=J_{t}
for
t\in[0,1]
in
\in\mathcal{J}
, the moduli space of Floer solutions
u:\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
between intersection points
x_{0}
and
x_{1}
in
\phi_{H}(\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}})\pitchfork\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}}

(3.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(x_{0},x_{1};J):=\{u:du^{0,1}=0,\lim_{s\to-\infty}u(s,t)=x_{0},\lim_{s\to\infty}u(s,t)=x_{1},u(s,0)\in\phi_{H}(\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}}),u(s,1)\in\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}}\}/\mathbb{R}

is transversely cut out and can be compactified to a topological manifold with corners.
The condition that
J
agrees with the complex structure of
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
near infinity guarantees that we can run Gromov compactness to compactify the moduli spaces.

The condition that
J
agrees with the complex structure of
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
near
D_{o}
, together with the fact that
D_{o}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
does not contain any
J
-holomorphic sphere, guarantees that every intersection between
u
and
D_{o}
is positive. This positivity in turn implies that we can define the differential to be

(3.6)

\displaystyle\partial x_{1}=\sum_{x_{0},|x_{0}|-|x_{1}|=1}\#\mathcal{M}(x_{0},x_{1};J)U^{u\cdot D_{o}}x_{0}

where
u\cdot D_{o}
is the algebraic intersection number between
u\in\mathcal{M}(x_{0},x_{1};J)
and
D_{o}
. This number is independent of the choice of
u\in\mathcal{M}(x_{0},x_{1};J)
, and moreover
2(u\cdot D_{o})
is equal to the relative winding grading
w(x_{0},x_{1})
.
We denote the homology with respect to the differential
d
by
HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{0},\EuScript{L}_{1};H)
, and let the
annular homology

HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{0},\EuScript{L}_{1};H)
be the homology of the truncated complex

CF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{0},\EuScript{L}_{1};H;\mathbb{F}[U])/(U=0):=CF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{0},\EuScript{L}_{1};H)\otimes_{\mathbb{F}[U]}\mathbb{F}.

In particular, the annular homology does not count disks passing through the divisor
D_{o}
. Both
HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{0},\EuScript{L}_{1};H;\mathbb{F}[U])
and
HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{0},\EuScript{L}_{1};H)
are independent of the choice of Hamiltonian
H
; we therefore drop
H
from the notation when the explicit choice is not important. Note furthermore that the differential of
CF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},L_{\underline{c}_{0}},L_{\underline{c}_{1}})
preserves the relative winding grading, and therefore
HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},L_{\underline{c}_{0}},L_{\underline{c}_{1}})
splits with respect to the relative winding grading.

With this in mind, we are ready to define our annular link invariant. Let
L
an annular link in
S^{3}
be the closure of a braid
b
identified with an element
\beta\in\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}\subseteq\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}
. As previously, let
\underline{c}_{up}
be a crossingless annular matching of
\tau_{o}=[-n,\dots,-1,1,\dots n]
in the upper half-plane which matches
-k
to
k
, as in Figure
3.3
.

Definition 3.5
.

The annular symplectic Khovanov homology of
L
over
\mathbb{F}
is

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{*}(L)\vcentcolon=HF_{ann}^{*+n+w}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}}).

Remark 3.6
.

For notational simplicity, we have introduced the annular symplectic Khovanov homology over fields of characteristic two; and indeed in this paper we will be almost exclusively concerned with the theory over
\mathbb{F}_{2}
the field with two elements. However, the Lagrangian
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
associated to a crossingless matching is diffeomorphic to a product of spheres, and is thus spin with a unique spin structure. Therefore, it is also straightforward to give coherent orientations to the moduli spaces of Floer solutions to define the symplectic annular Khovanov homology over the integers, and thus any field. The invariance proofs of Section
3.3
go through equally well in this case.

Remark 3.7
.

Note that the condition that
J
agrees with the complex structure of
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
near
D_{HC}\cap\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}
allows us to apply the tautological correspondence to a pseudoholomorphic strip
u
to get a pseudo-holomorphic map
(v,\pi_{\Sigma})\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\Sigma\to A_{\tau_{o}}\times(\mathbb{R}\times[0,1])
such that
\pi_{\Sigma}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\Sigma\to\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]
is an
n
-fold branched covering. See
[
AS16
, Section 5.8]
and
[
DS03
,
Smi03
]
, as well as
[
OS04b
, Lemma 3.6]
and
[
Lip06
, Section 13]
for further discussion of the tautological correspondence. This observation will be helpful for our applications.

3.3.
Invariance under Markov moves and other properties

In this section we establish invariance of our annular symplectic Khovanov homology under Markov moves, and discuss how to promote the relative winding grading constructed in the previous section to an annular grading. We then conclude by proving Theorem
1.2
.

Our invariance arguments closely follow those of
[
SS06
]
for ordinary symplectic Khovanov homology. We begin with the following modification of
[
SS06
, Lemma 49]
, showing invariance for handleslides among the components of a crossingless annular matching.

Lemma 3.8

(Handleslide invariance)
.

Let
\underline{c}=\{c_{1},\dots,c_{n}\}
be a crossingless annular matching. Suppose that
c_{1}^{\prime}
is an annular matching obtained from handlesliding
c_{1}
across
c_{2}
such that the handleslide region does not intersect the origin in
\in\mathbb{C}
. Define
\underline{c}^{\prime}:=\{c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2},\dots,c_{n}\}
.
Then the associated Lagrangian
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
through an isotopy in the complement of
D_{o}
. In particular, for any other crossingless annular matching
\underline{d}
, we have
HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{d}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}};\mathbb{F}[U])\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{d}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}};\mathbb{F}[U])
.

An example of such a handleslide is shown in Figure
3.2
.

Proof.

The proof of
[
SS06
, Lemma 49]
goes as follows.
Recall that the two endpoints of
c_{2}
are elements of
2n
-tuple
\tau_{0}
.
By moving the two endpoints towards each other along
c_{2}
until they coincide and adjoining the endpoints of
c_{j}
for
j\neq 2
, one obtains a path
(\tau_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
of
2n
-tuples starting at
\tau_{o}
and ending at a point in
\operatorname{Sym}^{2n}(\mathbb{C})\setminus\operatorname{Conf}^{2n}(\mathbb{C})
.
The corresponding family
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{t}}
gives a Morse-Bott degeneration from
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{0}}
to the singular space
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{1}}
, and
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
is the vanishing cycle of the Lagrangian
\EuScript{L}_{\{c_{1},c_{3},\dots,c_{n}\}}
in the critical locus of

\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{1}}
.
In the critical locus,
\EuScript{L}_{\{c_{1},c_{3},\dots,c_{n}\}}
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
\EuScript{L}_{\{c_{1}^{\prime},c_{3},\dots,c_{n}\}}
because
c_{1}
is homotopic to
c_{1}^{\prime}
when
c_{2}
(including the two endpoints of
c_{2}
) is absent.
The vanishing cycle of
\EuScript{L}_{\{c_{1}^{\prime},c_{3},\dots,c_{n}\}}
is
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
.
Since
\EuScript{L}_{\{c_{1},c_{3},\dots,c_{n}\}}
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
\EuScript{L}_{\{c_{1}^{\prime},c_{3},\dots,c_{n}\}}
and
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
and
L_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
are the respective vanishing cycles,
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
.

Since the path
(\tau_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
and the isotopy from
c_{1}
to
c_{1}^{\prime}
when
c_{2}
is absent are both away from the origin, one can show that the Hamiltonian isotopy from
L_{\underline{c}}
to
L_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
is away from
D_{o}
. ∎

Remark 3.9
.

For the remainder of the paper, we only need the weaker statement that
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
is Floer theoretically isomorphic to
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
, in the sense that
HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime\prime}};\mathbb{F}[U])=HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime\prime}};\mathbb{F}[U])
for any crossingless annular matching
\underline{c}^{\prime\prime}
.
One way to prove this is to show that there is an element
a\in HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}};\mathbb{F}[U])
and
a^{\prime}\in HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}};\mathbb{F}[U])
such that the Floer product
\mu_{2}(a,a^{\prime})
and
\mu_{2}(a^{\prime},a)
are the respective identity elements.
This can be proved by combining (i)
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
is Floer theoretically isomorphic to
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
when
U=1
, which is exactly
[
SS06
, Lemma 49]
, and (ii) for any pseudoholomorphic map contributing to the Floer product,
the projection of the corresponding pseudo-holomorphic map
\pi_{A}\circ v\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\Sigma\to\mathbb{C}
, as in Remark
3.7
, misses the origin because of the open mapping theorem.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 3.2
.
Two crossingless annular matchings
\underline{c}=(c_{1},c_{2})
and
\underline{c}^{\prime}=(c_{1}^{\prime},c_{2})
, with matching paths marked in blue, each avoiding the origin, marked with a green star. The path
c_{1}^{\prime}
is the result of sliding
c_{1}
over
c_{2}
, with handleslide region not intersecting the origin. The associated Lagrangians
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}}
and
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}^{\prime}}
are Lagrangian isotopic in
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}\setminus D_{o}
.

We now confirm invariance of the annular symplectic Khovanov homology under Markov conjugation. As in Section
3.2
, let
\underline{c}_{up}
be a crossingless annular matching for
\tau_{o}
in the upper-half plane which matches
-k
with
k
, as in Figure
3.3
. Our proof closely mimics
[
SS06
, Proposition 54]
.

Figure 3.3
.
A crossingless annular matching
\underline{c}_{up}
in the upper-half plane, with matching paths drawn in blue, avoiding the origin, marked with a green star.

Lemma 3.10

(Invariance under Markov conjugation)
.

For any braid
\beta\in\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}\subseteq\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}

HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\sigma_{j}\beta\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

for all
j=1,\dots,n-1
.

Proof.

We recall from
[
SS06
, Lemma 53]
that for
1\leq j\leq n
, the crossingless annular matching obtained by acting by
\sigma_{j}^{-1}\sigma_{2n-j}
on
\underline{c}_{up}
differs from
\underline{c}_{up}
by a handleslide away from the origin, as in Figure
3.4
. With this in mind, we have the following string of isomorphisms:

\displaystyle HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\sigma_{j}\beta\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\sigma_{2n-j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\sigma_{2n-j}\beta\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\sigma_{2n-j}\sigma_{j}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle\simeq HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U]).

Here the first and third isomorphisms are applications of invariance under symplectomorphisms preserving the divisor
D_{o}
, the second and fifth isomorphisms are applications of Lemma
3.8
, and the third isomorphism uses the fact that if
\beta\in\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}\subseteq\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}
and
1\leq j\leq n-1
then
\beta
commutes with
\sigma_{2n-j}
in
\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}
.
∎

Figure 3.4
.
The effect of acting on
\underline{c}_{up}
by
\sigma_{2n-j}\sigma_{j}^{-1}
is shown on the right in the case
j=1
and
n=2
. We observe that this is handleslide equivalent to
\underline{c}_{up}
, shown on the left.

We now prove invariance under Markov stabilization, following
[
SS06
, Proposition 55, Lemma 57]
. To distinguish
\tau_{o}
in different dimensions, let
\tau_{o,n}=\{-n,\dots,n\}
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Lemma 3.11

(Invariance under Markov stabilization)
.

For any
\beta_{1},\beta_{2}\in\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}

\displaystyle HF^{*}(\mathcal{Y}_{n+1,\tau_{o,n+1}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{1}\sigma_{n}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])\simeq HF^{*}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o,n}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle HF^{*+2}(\mathcal{Y}_{n+1,\tau_{o,n+1}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{1}\sigma_{n}^{-1}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])\simeq HF^{*}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o,n}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{1}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

Proof.

By symplectomorphism invariance, it suffices to prove that

\displaystyle HF^{*}(\mathcal{Y}_{n+1,\tau_{o,n+1}},\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\sigma_{n}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])\simeq HF^{*}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o,n}},\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

\displaystyle HF^{*+2}(\mathcal{Y}_{n+1,\tau_{o,n+1}},\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\sigma_{n}^{-1}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])\simeq HF^{*}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o,n}},\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])

Following the proof of
[
SS06
, Proposition 55]
, we wish to consider a Morse-Bott degeneration arising from colliding
\{-2,-1,1\}
. Toward this end, we can choose a path
\gamma\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu[0,1]\to\operatorname{Sym}^{2(n+1)}(\mathbb{C})
such that
\gamma(0)=\tau_{o,n+1}
, for all
t\in[0,1)
we have
\{-(n+1),\dots,-2,2,\dots,(n+1)\}\subseteq\gamma(t)\in\operatorname{Conf}^{2(n+1),0}(\mathbb{C})
, and finally

\gamma(1)=(-(n+1),\dots,-3,-2,-2,-2,2,3,\dots,n+1)\in\operatorname{Sym}^{2(n+1)}(\mathbb{C}).

In particular this path avoids the origin. The remainder of the argument goes through as in
[
SS06
, Proposition 55]
without alteration. More precisely, the critical locus of this degeneration is

\mathcal{Y}_{n,(-(n+1),\dots,-3,-2,2,3,\dots,n+1)}\simeq\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o,n}}.

and furthermore this identification sends the annular divisor of one side to the annular divisor of the other. Moreover, under this identification, the Lagrangian
\sigma_{n}^{\pm 1}\beta_{2}L_{\underline{c}_{up}}
and
\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}}
are vanishing cycles over

\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}}
and
\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}}
, respectively, with their fibred spheres transversely intersecting at
1
point.
This show that
HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n+1,\tau_{o,n+1}},\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\sigma_{n}^{\pm 1}\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])
is identified with the Floer cohomology inside the critical locus, which is
HF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o,n}},\beta_{1}^{-1}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta_{2}\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}};\mathbb{F}[U])
, up to a grading shift by
0
and
2
respectively for
\sigma_{n}
and
\sigma_{n}^{-1}
.
∎

We may now briefly complete the proof of Theorem
1.1
, establishing that
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
is a well-defined link invariant.

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Recall from Theorem
2.2
that two annular braids
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
have the same annular link closures if and only if they differ by a sequence of Markov conjugations and Markov stabilizations. Therefore invariance follows from Lemmas
3.10
and
3.11
. Moreover, since the isomorphisms of Lemmas
3.10
and
3.11
) preserve the relative winding grading, there is a well-defined relative winding grading on
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
; the theory therefore decomposes into a direct sum along the relative winding grading, every direct summand of which is an invariant of
L
.
∎

We conclude the discussion of invariance with the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Proof.

We observe that
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
is the homology of the associated graded of the filtration induced by the (relative) winding grading on
CF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}})
, the chain complex computing
\operatorname{Kh}_{symp}(L)
. The existence of the spectral sequence of Theorem
1.2
therefore follows from standard homological algebra. Moreover, the maps of Lemmas
3.10
and
3.11
induce filtered chain homotopy equivalences on
CF(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}},\beta\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{up}})
, from which follows invariance of every page of the spectral sequence.
∎

Remark 3.12
.

We remind the reader that so far we have used the Kähler form restricted from
\mathbb{C}^{4n}
.
As noted at the end of Section
3.1
, a direct generalization of
[
Man06
, Theorem 1.2]
show that we can compute the Floer cohomology and hence the symplectic annular Khovanov homology using
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}
instead, with respect to which the Lagrangians are isotopic to products of spheres.

We now discuss a useful alternate construction of
HF_{ann}
, in which we delete the divisor
D_{o}
instead of counting its intersections with pseudoholomorphic strips. Denote
A_{\tau}\setminus\{z=0\}
by
A_{\tau}^{*}
, which is a hypersurface in
\mathbb{C}^{2}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
.
The standard Kähler form on
\mathbb{C}^{2}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}
is
-dd^{c}(|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\log|z|)
, which restricts to a Kähler form
\omega_{A^{*}}
on
A_{\tau}^{*}
. There exists a Kähler form
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}_{o}}
on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*})
which agrees with the product form of
\omega_{A^{*}}
away from a small neighborhood of
D_{HC}
by the same argument as
[
AS19
, Lemma 5.5]
. With respect to this form, we can consider the Lagrangian Floer cohomology

(3.7)

HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}},\Sigma_{\underline{d}})

where
\Sigma_{\underline{c}}
is the product of the matching spheres for the matching paths
c_{i}\in\underline{c}
as in (
3.4
) and likewise
\Sigma_{\underline{d}}
.

As a preparation for the following lemma, we recall that:

Definition 3.13
.

A Stein homotopy on a smooth manifold
X
is a family of Stein structures
(J_{t},\phi_{t})_{t\in[0,1]}
, where
J_{t}
is a complex structure and
\phi_{t}
is an exhausting plurisubharmonic function, such that the critical points of
\phi_{t}
do not go to the infinity during the homotopy.
In this case, we call
(J_{0},\phi_{0})
Stein homotopy equivalent to
(J_{1},\phi_{1})
.
A Stein deformation equivalence between
(X_{0},J_{0},\phi_{0})
and
(X_{1},J_{1},\phi_{1})
consists of a diffeomorphism
f:X_{0}\to X_{1}
and a Stein homotopy equivalence from
(J_{0},\phi_{0})
to
(f^{*}J_{1},f^{*}\phi_{1})
.

A Stein homotopy induces a symplectomorphism between the associated Kähler forms
-d(d\phi_{0}\circ J_{0})
and
-d(d\phi_{1}\circ J_{1})
. More discussion of Liouville/Weinstein/Stein homotopy can be found in
[
CE12
, Chapter 11]
.

Lemma 3.14
.

The Lagrangian Floer cohomology in the horizontal Hilbert scheme with the divisor deleted (
3.7
) agrees with
HF_{ann}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}},\Sigma_{\underline{d}})
. Therefore, given an annular link
L
which is the closure of a braid
\beta\in\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}
, we have that
HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{up}},\Sigma_{\beta\underline{c}_{up}})
agrees with
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
as defined in Definition
3.5
.

Sketch of proof.

Recall that
\omega_{A}
is the standard Kähler form on
A_{\tau}
.
For any small neighborhood
N
of
\pi_{A}^{-1}(0)=\{z=0\}\subseteq A_{\tau}
, we can construct a Kähler form
\omega^{\prime}
on
A_{\tau}^{*}
which agrees with
\omega_{A}|_{A_{\tau}^{*}}
outside
N
such that
(A_{\tau}^{*},\omega^{\prime})
is Stein and Stein homotopy equivalent to
(A_{\tau}^{*},\omega_{A^{*}})
. Note that we do not change the complex structure during the homotopy. For example,
\omega^{\prime}
can be constructed using a Kähler potential of the form

|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}+|z|^{2}+\rho(|z|)\log|z|
for some cutoff function
\rho
supported in a small neighborhood of
0
. One may then use this to produce a Kähler form
\omega^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}}
on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*})
which agrees with
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}
away from an arbitrarily small neighborhood
N_{o}
of
D_{o}
. Now, for any pair of product Lagrangians associated to matchings
\Sigma_{\underline{c}}
and
\Sigma_{\underline{d}}
, there is such an arbitrarily small neighborhood
N_{o}
of
D_{o}
such that all the Floer solutions contributing to
HF_{ann}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}},\Sigma_{\underline{d}})
do not pass through
N_{o}
. Therefore,
HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}},\Sigma_{\underline{d}})
computed using
\omega^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}}
agrees with
HF_{ann}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}},\Sigma_{\underline{d}})
. Since
(A_{\tau}^{*},\omega^{\prime})
is Stein homotopy equivalent to
(A_{\tau}^{*},\omega_{A^{*}})
, it follows that the same is true if we replace
\omega^{\prime}_{\mathcal{Y}}
with
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}_{o}}
.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
3.14
, we will use the two constructions of symplectic annular Khovanov homology interchangeably.

3.3.1.
The absolute winding grading

The first application of this new variant of symplectic annular Khovanov homology, in which we delete the divisor
D_{o}
, is to promote the relative winding grading on symplectic Khovanov homology to an absolute grading. The following discussion is inspired by
[
GLW18
, Lemma 2]
. We can compactify
\mathbb{C}
to
\mathbb{CP}^{1}
by adding a point
\{\infty\}
at infinity.
Let
d_{\infty}
be the corresponding divisor of
\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1})
, that is, let
d_{\infty}=\{\infty\}\times\operatorname{Sym}^{n-1}(\mathbb{CP}^{1})
. As previously, we have an intersection pairing

\cdot d_{\infty}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\pi_{2}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}),\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0})\cup\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{1}))\to\mathbb{Z}.

We can define the relative winding grading at infinity in exactly the same way as Definition
3.4
by
considering
u_{\infty}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
with the same boundary and asymptotic conditions and replacing
d_{0}
by
d_{\infty}
. Given two intersection points
x_{0},x_{1}\in\phi_{H}(\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}})\pitchfork\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}}
, this relative grading is written
w_{\infty}(x_{0},x_{1})
.

Lemma 3.15
.

For any
x_{0},x_{1}\in\phi_{H}(\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}})\pitchfork\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}}
, the relative winding grading at infinity is the opposite of the relative winding grading; that is,
w(x_{0},x_{1})=-w_{\infty}(x_{0},x_{1})
.

Proof.

Let
u_{\infty}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}\setminus\{0\})
be as above and
u\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{R}\times[0,1]\to\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{C})
be as in Definition
3.4
.
Precompose
u_{\infty}
with multiplication by
-1
on
\mathbb{R}
so that the result has the opposite orientation and call it
\bar{u}_{\infty}
.
Since
\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{1})
is contractible, we can homotope
\bar{u}_{\infty}
such that its boundary on
\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{1})
agrees with that of
u
.
We can then glue
\bar{u}_{\infty}
with
u
along the boundary to obtain a strip
\bar{u}_{\infty}\#u
which represents a class in
\pi_{2}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}),\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0}))
.
Note that
\pi_{2}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}),\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0}))=\pi_{2}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}))=\pi_{2}(\mathbb{CP}^{n})=\mathbb{Z}
.
Since the divisor
d_{0}
is homotopic to
d_{\infty}
away from
\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0})
, the intersection pairing

\cdot(d_{o}-d_{\infty}):\pi_{2}(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\mathbb{CP}^{1}),\operatorname{Sym}(\underline{c}_{0}))\to\mathbb{Z}

is the zero map.
Therefore, we have

0=[\bar{u}_{\infty}\#u]\cdot(d_{o}-d_{\infty})=[u_{\infty}]\cdot d_{\infty}+[u]\cdot d_{o}

from which the result follows.
∎

Corollary 3.16
.

There is a vector space isomorphism
R\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\to\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
which negates the relative winding grading.

Proof.

Let
\bar{f}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{C}^{*}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}
be the map
z\mapsto\frac{1}{z}
.
Let
\tau\in\operatorname{Conf}^{2n}(\mathbb{C})
be chosen such that it is preserved by
\bar{f}
.
Let
f\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muA_{\tau}^{*}\to A_{\tau}^{*}
be given by
(u,v,z)\mapsto(u,v,\frac{1}{z})
. Observe that this is a symplectomorphism with respect to the restriction of
-dd^{c}(|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\log|z|)
to
A_{\tau}^{*}
.
In particular, it induces an isomorphism

CF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}})\to CF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau},\EuScript{L}_{\bar{f}(\underline{c}_{0})},\EuScript{L}_{\bar{f}(\underline{c}_{1})})

for appropriate choices of almost complex structures and Hamiltonian perturbations.

If we choose
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}}
and
\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}}
such that
HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}})
computes symplectic annular Khovanov homology
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
, then

HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau},\EuScript{L}_{\bar{f}(\underline{c}_{0})},\EuScript{L}_{\bar{f}(\underline{c}_{1})})
also computes
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
because the annular link does not change under applying
f
.
However, the relative winding grading of two elements in
HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{0}},\EuScript{L}_{\underline{c}_{1}})
is negated under the map to
HF_{ann}(\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau},\EuScript{L}_{\bar{f}(\underline{c}_{0})},\EuScript{L}_{\bar{f}(\underline{c}_{1})})
by Lemma
3.15
.
∎

We may use Corollary
3.16
to establish an absolute winding grading: if
w(x_{0},R(x_{0}))=2k
, then we set the absolute grading of
x_{0}
to be
k
. This gives us a unique way to write the annular symplectic Khovanov homology symmetrically as a direct sum decomposition

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\simeq\oplus_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L;k)

such that
w(x_{0},x_{1})=k_{0}-k_{1}
for
x_{i}\in\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L;k_{i})
.

Definition 3.17
.

The absolute winding grading
w(x)
for
x\in\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L;k)
is the integer
k
.

3.3.2.
Relationship to other theories

In this section we summarize the expected relationship of our annular symplectic Khovanov homology to other theories, and explain the motivation for its definition. Recall from Remark
3.6
that, although we have taken coefficients in
\mathbb{F}_{2}
for convenience,
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
is definable over any field.

Conjecture 3.18
.

Over a field
\mathbb{F}
of characteristic zero, there is an isomorphism

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{i}(L;k;\mathbb{F})\simeq\oplus_{j-q=i}\operatorname{AKh}^{j,q}(L;k;\mathbb{F})

in analogy with that of Abouzaid and Smith
[
AS16
,
AS19
]
for the non-annular case. Here
i
denotes the absolute homological grading on the symplectic side, which we will refer to as the Khovanov grading where there is any possibility of ambiguity. Meanwhile
(j,q)
are the homological and quantum gradings on the combinatorial side, and
k
is the winding or annular grading. If
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
is equipped with a relative quantum (equivariant) grading from a non-commutative vector field as in
[
Che23
]
, then this isomorphism may be upgraded to an isomorphism of (relatively) trigraded groups.

In particular, Conjecture
3.18
implies that our theory agrees with the Mak-Smith formulation
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{HH}(L)
over a field of characteristic zero, which is to say wherever both are defined.

The techniques used in Sections
3.2
and
3.3
to introduce annular symplectic Khovanov homology and prove its invariance are essentially standard; it is well-known to the experts that one could in principle define a link invariant by following our recipe. However, a priori there would be no reason to believe that the result was related to combinatorial annular Khovanov homology. We now explain the motivation for Conjecture
3.18
. Many annular link invariants including the annular Khovanov homology are governed by cylindrical Khovanov-Lauda-Rouquier-Webster algebras. It is expected that the Koszul dual of the KLRW algebra agrees with the Fukaya algebra of a collection of basic Lagrangians, including
L_{\underline{c}_{up}}
, in
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}\setminus D_{o}
, and the way that the Koszul dual algebra governs the annular Khovanov homology agrees with the way that the Fukaya algebra governs the symplectic annular Khovanov homology. (We are grateful to Ben Webster for early explanations helpful to understanding the conjectural correspondence.) For more on the non-cylindrical KLRW algebras, we direct the reader to
[
KL09
,
KL11
,
Web19
,
Rou08
]
. For more on their relation to link invariants and specifically the annular case, see
[
Web17
]
. Finally,
[
QRS18
]
discusses the categorification of annular link invariants, in terms of an algebra expected to be Morita equivalent to the KLRW algebra.

A related conjecture has also appeared in recent work of Aganagic, Danilenko, Li, Shende, Zhou
[
ADL+24
, Conjecture 1.8]
. We expect that
\mathcal{Y}_{n,\tau_{o}}\setminus D_{o}
is symplectomorphic to the symplectic manifold
\mathcal{M}^{\times}(\Gamma,\vec{d})
considered in their paper. If Conjecture 1.8 of
[
ADL+24
]

is true, then the Fukaya algebra of the collection of Lagrangians they consider will be Koszul dual to the Fukaya algebra of the collection of basic Lagrangians we use, giving an approach towards proving our symplectic annular Khovanov homology agrees with the combinatorial version.

Alternatively, one could try to relate
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{HH}(L)
and
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
directly without passing through the combinatorial version. When
L
is the annular braid closure of a braid with
n
strands in
\mathbb{R}^{2}\times[0,1]
, the symplectic manifold involved in defining
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
is
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A^{*}_{\tau})
with
\tau\in\operatorname{Conf}^{2n}(\mathbb{C})
.
On the other hand, the symplectic manifolds involved in defining
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{HH}(L)
are
\operatorname{Hilb}^{k,hor}(A_{\tau^{\prime}})
with
\tau^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Conf}^{n}(\mathbb{C})
and
k=1,\dots,n
.
In
[
MS22
, Section 12]
, the authors define a full and faithful functor from the Fukaya-Seidel category of

\operatorname{Hilb}^{k,hor}(A_{\tau^{\prime}})\times\operatorname{Hilb}^{n-k,hor}(A_{\tau^{\prime}})
to the Fukaya-Seidel category of
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau})
.
The same construction gives us a full and faithful functor to the Fukaya-Seidel category of
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*})
.
Applying the reasoning of
[
MS22
, Section 12]
, we obtain a spectral sequence which converges to
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
whose second page is a direct sum of Hochschild homology of bimodules. We expect that the second page is in fact exactly
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{HH}(L)
, similarly to how the spectral sequence from
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{HH}(L)
to
\operatorname{Kh}_{symp}(L)
is obtained in
[
MS22
, Section 12]
, and we conjecture that the spectral sequence collapses such that the second page, so that
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}^{HH}(L)
agrees with the last page,
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
.

3.4.
Two
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-quotients

In this section, we introduce two additional symplectic annular link invariants, each of which is isomorphic to symplectic annular Khovanov homology. These will play crucial roles in the proofs of Theorems
1.5
and
1.7
in Sections
5
and
6
respectively.

Let
\iota\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{C}\to\mathbb{C}
be the involution given by
z\mapsto-z
.
Let
\iota_{A},\iota_{B}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{C}^{3}\to\mathbb{C}^{3}
be the maps

\iota_{A}(u,v,z)=(u,v,-z)\qquad\qquad\iota_{B}(u,v,z)=(-u,-v,-z).

Given
\tau\in\operatorname{Conf}^{4n,0}
which is fixed by the action induced by
\iota
on the configuration space, the involutions
\iota_{A},\iota_{B}
restrict to involutions on
A_{\tau}
. Moreover, these involutions further restrict to two free involutions on
A^{*}_{\tau}:=A_{\tau}\setminus\{z=0\}
. We denote the quotients of
A^{*}_{\tau}
with respect to these two actions by
Q_{A,\tau}
and
Q_{B,\tau}
respectively.

The fibration
\pi_{A}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muA_{\tau}^{*}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}
commutes with each of the involutions
\iota_{A}
and
\iota_{B}
, so we have induced fibrations

\pi_{Q,A}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muQ_{A,\tau}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}/\langle\iota\rangle\qquad\qquad\pi_{Q,B}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muQ_{B,\tau}\to\mathbb{C}^{*}/\langle\iota\rangle.

By varying
\tau\in\operatorname{Conf}^{4n,0}\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
, we obtain monodromy actions

\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}\to\mathrm{Symp}(Q_{A,\tau})/\mathrm{Ham}(Q_{A,\tau})\qquad\qquad\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}\to\mathrm{Symp}(Q_{B,\tau})/\mathrm{Ham}(Q_{B,\tau}).

Let
\tau_{Q,o}
denote the configuration
\{-2n,\dots,-1,1,\dots,2n\}
. We consider the subgroup
\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}\subseteq\operatorname{Br}_{1,2n}
corresponding to treating the image of
\tau_{Q,o}
under the quotient map as a basepoint and varying
\tau
among configurations fixed by
\iota
which contain
\{-2n,\dots,-n-1\}\cup\{n+1,\dots,2n\}
and for which the remaining points
z
in the configuration have
Re(z)\in[-n-0.5,n+0.5]
. In particular, the only elements of
\tau_{Q,o}
which may vary are
\{-n,\dots,-1,1,\dots,n\}
.

With respect to the fibrations
\pi_{Q,A}
and
\pi_{Q,B}
, we can form the horizontal Hilbert schemes analogously to (
3.2
), written as

\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau})\qquad\qquad\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{B,\tau}).

Where it is clear that
\tau=\tau_{Q,o}
, we omit the subscripts from the horizontal Hilbert scheme of the quotient for notational simplicity.

Let
\underline{c}_{Q,up}=\{c_{1},\dots,c_{n}\}
denote the set of
n
annular matching paths on the upper half plane which match the point
k+1
with the point
2n-k
for
k=0,\dots,n-1
, as in Figure
3.5
. Let

\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{Q,up}}=\Sigma_{c_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{c_{n}}

be the isotropic submanifold of
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}})
which is constructed by taking the product of the spheres
\Sigma_{c_{i}}
associated to each of the
n
paths
c_{i}
, where
\Sigma_{c_{i}}
is the matching sphere of (
3.3
). The image of
\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{Q,up}}
under each of the quotient maps to
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau})
and
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{B,\tau})
is then Lagrangian. Denote these resulting Lagrangians by
\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{Q,A,up}}
and
\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{Q,B,up}}
respectively.

Figure 3.5
.
The set of
n
annular matching paths
\underline{c}_{Q,up}
in the upper-half plane, drawn in blue, which avoid the origin, marked with a green star. These paths may be used to construct a Lagrangian in the horizontal Hilbert scheme of either quotient of
A_{\tau}
.

Using the induced
\operatorname{Br}_{1,n}
actions on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau})
and
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{B,\tau})
respectively, given an annular link
L
represented by an
n
-strand annular braid
\beta
, we may define

\operatorname{AKh}^{*}_{Q,A/B}(L):=HF^{*+n+w}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A/B,\tau}),\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{Q,A/B,up}},\beta\Sigma_{\underline{c}_{Q,A/B,up}}).

Note that the theory above is standard Lagrangian Floer cohomology over a field of characteristic two, as we have already removed the preimage of zero from
A_{\tau}
before constructing the various symplectic manifolds involved. Here, as in Lemma
3.14
and surrounding discussion, we use the Kähler form
\omega_{A^{*}}=-dd^{c}(|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\log|z|)
on
A_{\tau}^{*}
and the resulting quotient symplectic form on
Q_{A/B,\tau}
.

The main result of this section is the following.

Proposition 3.19
.

For any annular link
L
, we have

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(L)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,B}(L)
.

The proof of Proposition
3.19
will occupy the rest of the section.

3.4.1.
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(L)

We begin by checking that for any annular link
L
, the first of our new annular invariants agrees with our annular symplectic Khovanov homology. Given
\tau=\{\pm\tau_{1},\dots,\pm\tau_{2n}\}
a configuation in
\operatorname{Conf}^{4n,0}(\mathbb{C})\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
, set
\tau^{2}=\{\tau_{1}^{2},\dots\tau_{2n}^{2}\}\in\operatorname{Conf}^{2n}(\mathbb{C})
.

Lemma 3.20
.

There is a biholomorphism from
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})/\langle\iota_{A}\rangle
to
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})

which restricts to a
biholomorphism from
Q_{A,\tau}
to
A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}
.

Proof.

Consider the holomorphic map

\phi\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}\to\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z},\qquad(u,v,z)\mapsto(u,v,z^{2}).

We have
\phi\circ\iota_{A}=\phi
, so it follows that
\phi
descends to a holomorphic map
from
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})/\langle\iota_{A}\rangle

to
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})
, which is clearly a biholomorphism. Since
\phi|_{A^{*}_{\tau}}
is a holomorphic map onto
A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}
, it descends to a biholomorphism from
Q_{A,\tau}
to
A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}
.
∎

Recall the definition of Stein homotopy/deformation equivalence from Definition
3.13
.

Corollary 3.21
.

Q_{A,\tau}
is Stein deformation equivalent to
A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}
. As a result, for any annular link
L
we have
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(L)
.

Proof.

Let
\omega_{A^{*}}=-dd^{c}(|u|^{2}+|v|^{2}+\log|z|)=-dd^{c}(\rho)
be the standard symplectic form on
\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}
.
For
t\in[0,1]
, let
\rho_{t}:=(1-t)\rho+t\phi^{*}\rho
.
Since any convex linear combination of exhausting plurisubharmonic functions is an exhausting plurisubharmonic function,
the family
\rho_{t}
gives a family of
\iota_{A}
-invariant Stein structures on
\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}
.
Let

\omega_{t}=(1-t)\omega_{A^{*}}+t\phi^{*}\omega_{A^{*}}=-dd^{c}((1-t)\rho+t\phi^{*}\rho)

be the associated family of Kähler forms.
It is routine to check that the critical points of
\rho_{t}
do not go to infinity so
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z},\omega_{0})
is
\iota_{A}
-equivariantly Stein homotopy equivalent to
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z},\omega_{1})
.
The space
Q_{A,\tau}
is a complex hypersurface of the
\iota_{A}
-quotient of
\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}
.
Restricting the Stein homotopy to
Q_{A,\tau}
shows that
(Q_{A,\tau},\omega_{0}|_{Q_{A,\tau}})

is Stein homotopy equivalent to
(Q_{A,\tau},\omega_{1}|_{Q_{A,\tau}})
, which is isomorphic to
(A^{*}_{\tau^{2}},\omega_{A^{*}}|_{A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}})
via
\phi
.
Therefore,
(Q_{A,\tau},\omega_{0}|_{Q_{A,\tau}})
, which is equipped with the Stein structure descended from

(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z},\omega_{A^{*}})
, is Stein deformation equivalent to
(A^{*}_{\tau^{2}},\omega_{A^{*}}|_{A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}})
.

Finally, we want to compare the Lagrangian matching spheres in
(Q_{A,\tau},\omega_{t}|_{Q_{A,\tau}})
for
t\in[0,1]

with respect to the Lefschetz fibration to
\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}/(z=-z)
.
For each
t_{0},t_{1}\in[0,1]
, the Stein homotopy from
t=t_{0}
to
t=t_{1}
induces a symplectomorphism
\psi_{t_{0},t_{1}}
from
\omega_{t_{0}}
to
\omega_{t_{1}}
.
For any matching path
c
, denote the Lagrangian matching sphere with respect to
\omega_{t}
by
\mathcal{L}_{c,t}
.
When
t_{0}
is close to
t_{1}
,
\psi_{t_{0},t_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{0}})
is
C^{1}
close to
\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{1}}
.
In particular,
\psi_{t_{0},t_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{0}})
is a graph with respect to a Weinstein neighborhood of
\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{1}}
.
Therefore we see that
\psi_{t_{0},t_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{0}})
is Lagrangian isotopic to
\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{1}}
when
t_{0}
is close to
t_{1}
, and hence in fact Hamiltonian isotopic since spheres are simply connected
3
3

3

In fact, the nearby Lagrangian conjecture for
T^{*}S^{2}
is known
[
Hin04
]
so to conclude that
\psi_{t_{0},t_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{0}})
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{1}}
, we only need that
\psi_{t_{0},t_{1}}(\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{0}})
is
C^{0}
close to
\mathcal{L}_{c,t_{1}}
when
t_{0}
is close to
t_{1}
.
.
By the compactness of
[0,1]
, we know that
\psi_{0,1}(\mathcal{L}_{c,0})
is Hamiltonian isotopic to
\mathcal{L}_{c,1}
.
Since the matching spheres of
(Q_{A,\tau},\omega_{1}|_{Q_{A,\tau}})
can be identified with the matching spheres of
(A^{*}_{\tau^{2}},\omega_{A^{*}}|_{A^{*}_{\tau^{2}}})
via
\phi
, this proves that
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(L)
.

∎

3.4.2.
\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(\kappa)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,B}(\kappa)

We now check that the two new annular link invariants of this subsection agree.

Lemma 3.22
.

There is a biholomorphism from
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})/\langle\iota_{A}\rangle

to
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{u,v}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})/\langle\iota_{B}\rangle
which restricts to a
biholomorphism from
Q_{A,\tau}
to
Q_{B,\tau}
.

Proof.

It is easier to define the biholomorphism after a change of coordinates.
Let
x=u-iv
and
y=u+iv
so that
u^{2}+v^{2}=xy
.
With respect to these new coordinates, we have

\iota_{A}(x,y,z)=(x,y,-z),\qquad\qquad\iota_{B}(x,y,z)=(-x,-y,-z).

Moreover, with respect to these coordinates we have

A^{*}_{\tau}=\{xy=(z-\tau_{1})\dots(z-\tau_{2n})(z+\tau_{1})\dots(z+\tau_{2n})\}.

Consider the biholomorphic map

\phi\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0mu\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,y}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z}\to\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,y}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z},\qquad\qquad(x,y,z)\mapsto(xz,yz^{-1},z).

Now
\phi\circ\iota_{A}=\iota_{B}\circ\phi
, hence
\phi
descends to a biholomorphism
from
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,y}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})/\langle\iota_{A}\rangle

to
(\mathbb{C}^{2}_{x,y}\times\mathbb{C}^{*}_{z})/\langle\iota_{B}\rangle
. As
\phi|_{A^{*}_{\tau}}
is a biholomorphism onto
A^{*}_{\tau}
, it also descends to a biholomorphism from
Q_{A,\tau}
to
Q_{B,\tau}
.
∎

Corollary 3.23
.

Q_{A,\tau}
is Stein deformation equivalent to
Q_{B,\tau}
. As a result, for any annular link
L
, we have
\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(L)\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,B}(L)
.

Proof.

We may repeat the proof of Corollary
3.21
with the map
\phi
of that proof replaced by the map
\phi
of Lemma
3.22
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
3.19
.

This follows from combining the isomorphisms of Corollaries
3.21
and
3.23
.
∎

3.5.
Manolescu’s reformulation, bridge diagrams, and actions

In this subsection we review important features of Manolescu’s reformulation
[
Man06
]
of symplectic Khovanov homology, already introduced in Section
3.1
, and its relationship to bridge diagrams and actions, and discuss their generalization to the annular case. Note that
[
Man06
]
deals only with flattened braid diagrams, but it was checked by Waldron
[
Wal09
, Section 4.2]
that the construction works for general bridge diagrams. His arguments apply straightforwardly to the annular case.

Recall that a bridge diagram is a decomposition of a link into the union of two trivial tangles. For our purposes, the notation for bridge diagrams will be as follows. Let
\tau=\{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{2n}\}
be such that each
\tau_{i}
lies on the real line and we have
\tau_{i}>\tau_{i+1}
for
1\leq i\leq 2n
. Our usual basepoint
\tau_{o}
is an example of this. We will usually further require that there is some
j
such that
\tau_{2j}>0>\tau_{2j+1}
, which is to say there are an even number of negative
\tau_{i}
and an even number of positive
\tau_{i}
. Consider the crossingless matching
\underline{b}
consisting of line segments
b_{i}
on the real line joining
\tau_{2i-1}
to
\tau_{2i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
, which is annular if additional requirement on the configuration is imposed; these line segments are the
bridges
. We form a link diagram by adding a second crossingless annular matching
\underline{a}
consisting of
n
matching paths between the elements
\tau_{i}
in
\tau
, insisting that the interiors of the matching paths intersect transversely, the
a_{i}
undercross the
b_{i}
, and no
a_{i}
intersects the origin. The result is an (annular) link diagram for a link
L
. For examples, see Figures
5.1
,
5.2
,
6.4
,
6.5
,
6.6
, and
6.7
.

Recall that we may compute symplectic Khovanov homology in
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau})
using the restriction of the Kähler form
\omega_{\mathcal{Y}}
, with Lagrangians which are products of spheres. In particular, if given a matching path
c
,
\Sigma_{c}
is the Lagrangian sphere in
A_{\tau}
defined in (
3.3
), then we have Lagrangians

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{b_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{b_{n}}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{a_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{a_{n}}.

With this in mind one then has
\operatorname{Kh}_{symp}(L)\simeq HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}),\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta})
. In the annular case, we treat
L
as an annular link such that the origin is the intersection of the annular axis with
\mathbb{C}
. We may as previously let
A_{\tau}^{*}=A_{\tau}\setminus\{z=0\}
, such that
D_{o}
is exactly the complement of
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,\tau}(\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta})
. The intersection counting theory
HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}),\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta};\mathbb{F}_{2}[U])
agrees with that constructed previously, such that its annular truncation
HF_{ann}(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}),\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta})
is again annular symplectic Khovanov homology. If we delete the divisor and compute with the symplectic form of Lemma
3.14
, we equivalently obtain

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\simeq HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*}),\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta}).

The manifolds involved in symplectic Khovanov homology carry an
O(2)
action, which is especially easy to see from this perspective. The action of
O(2)
on
(u,v)\subset\mathbb{C}^{2}
induces a symplectic action on
A_{\tau}
, which fixes the Lagrangian sphere
\Sigma_{c}
for any matching path
c
. The induced action on the Hilbert scheme preserves
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau})
and indeed
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*})

[
HLS16
, Lemma 7.3]
, and fixes the Lagrangians. A candidate
O(2)
-equivariant symplectic Khovanov homology has been constructed by
the first author, Lipshitz, Sarkar
[
HLS20
]
.

Finally, note that
\Sigma_{\beta}
and
\Sigma_{\alpha}
intersect in a circle above any intersection between a path
b_{i}
and a path
a_{j}
. Typically one chooses a Hamiltonian perturbation of
\Sigma_{\beta}
such that this intersection becomes finite and consists of the generators for a Morse complex for the circle. In most cases, it suffices to use a complex with one generator in degree zero and one generator in degree one. If
\tau=\tau_{o}
, this perturbation may be carried out so as to result in a Lagrangian
\Sigma_{\beta}^{\prime}
which is preserved by the action induced by
\iota_{A}(u,v,z)=(u,v,-z)
on the Hilbert scheme, as in
[
HLS16
, Lemma 7.5]
. If we instead are interested in the involution
\iota_{B}(u,v,z)=(-u,-v,-z)
, we must instead choose a Morse complex with two generators in each of degrees zero and one, which again allows us to carry out the perturbation such that
\Sigma_{\beta}^{\prime}
remains preserved by the induced action on the Hilbert scheme. In either case, this explicit choice of perturbation makes it possible to list the generators of a chain complex computing
\operatorname{Kh}_{symp}
or
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}
starting from a bridge diagram of the link, and to use the tautological correspondence of Remark
3.7
to restrict the list of possible differentials on the complex.

4.
Symplectic annular Khovanov homology and link Floer homology in the branched cover

In this section we prove Theorem
1.3
, establishing a symplectic version the Roberts’ annular refinement of the Ozsváth-Szabó spectral sequence from Khovanov homology to Heegaard Floer homology. Toward this end, we recall the relationship of Manolescu’s reformulation of symplectic Khovanov homology with Heegaard Floer homology
[
Man06
,
SS10
]
.

Let
L
be an annular link with annular axis
A
. Consider an annular bridge diagram for
L
with notation as in Section
3.5
, so that the endpoints of the bridges lie at points of
\tau=\{\tau_{1},\dots\tau_{2n}\}\in\operatorname{Conf}^{2n,0}(\mathbb{C})
such that
\tau_{2i-1}
and
\tau_{2i}
are connected by the bridge
b_{i}
along the real axis in
\mathbb{C}
not crossing the origin, and the undercrossing arcs are labelled
a_{i},\dots,a_{n}
in some order. We may form the Riemann surface

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\tau}=\{(u,0,z)\in\mathbb{C}^{3}:u^{2}+p(z)=0\}\subseteq A_{\tau}

which is the branched cover of
\mathbb{C}\cup\{\infty\}
of
\mathbb{C}
over the
2n
points in
\tau
. We may compatify
\Sigma_{\tau}^{\prime}
to a surface
\Sigma_{\tau}
by filling in the two punctures at infinity to obtain a closed surface of genus
n-1
. We mark the the two preimages of
\{\infty\}
with basepoints labeled
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
and the two preimages of
\{0\}
with basepoints labeled
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
, so that
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\}
is the double branched cover of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}
over the set of
2n
points
\tau
. Note that Manolescu calls this surface
\Sigma_{0}
; to avoid confusion in the annular context, we use
\Sigma_{\tau}
.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 4.1
.
The double branched cover of the annular bridge diagram shown in Figure
5.1
along the endpoints of the bridges. Only the half of each of the red and orange curves
\widetilde{a}_{1}
and
\widetilde{a}_{2}
on the top side of the Heegaard surface facing the reader is shown; the remaining portions of the curve may be obtained by symmetry. The branched covering action is the hyperelliptic involution.

The preimage of each bridge
b_{i}
is a simple closed curve
\widehat{\beta}_{i}
on
\Sigma_{\tau}
, given by

\widehat{\beta}_{i}=\{(u,0,z):z\in b_{i},u\in\pm\sqrt{p(z)}\}.

Let
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
denote this set of
n
non-intersecting curves. Likewise, the preimage of each
a_{i}
is a simple closed curve
\widehat{\alpha}_{i}
and we may consider the set
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
. An example of an annular bridge diagram appears in Figure
5.1
; the corresponding surface
\Sigma_{\tau}
together with the decorations we have introduced is shown in Figure
4.1
.

Manolescu
[
Man06
, Proposition 7.4]
shows that
\mathcal{H}^{\prime}=(\Sigma_{0},\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}},\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}},\{w_{1},w_{2}\})
is a multipointed Heegaard diagram for
\Sigma(L)
. Since
\Sigma(mL)\simeq-\Sigma(L)
, it follows that that using the cohomologial conventions of this paper the Lagrangian Floer cochain complex
CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{0}\backslash\{w_{1},w_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\alpha}},\mathbb{T}_{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
computes the Heegaard Floer cohomology

\widehat{\mathit{HF}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L)\#(S^{1}\times S^{2}))\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HF}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L))\otimes H^{1}(S^{1})\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HF}}(\Sigma(mL))\otimes V.

We claim that the set of data
\mathcal{H}=(\Sigma_{\tau},\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}},\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}},\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\})
is a multi-pointed Heegaard diagram for
(\Sigma(L),\widetilde{A})
, where as in the introduction
\widetilde{A}
denotes the lift of the annular axis
A
under the map
\Sigma(L)\rightarrow S^{3}
. We must first check that each component of
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
contains exactly one
w_{i}
and one
z_{i}
, and similarly for
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
. This follows because both sets of curves
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
and
\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
cut the surface into two components interchanged by the branched covering involution. Since the points
w_{1}
and
w_{2}
are interchanged by the branched covering involution, they must lie in different components in
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
and also in
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
; the same is true of the points
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
.

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 4.2
.
The knot defined by the Heegaard diagram shown in Figure
4.1
, with its projection to the bridge diagram as the axis of the annular link shown in purple. In this diagram, we have drawn the knot upstairs with regard for visibility, such that it is not preserved by the branch covering action, but it may easily be redrawn as exactly the lift of the purple axis downstairs.

This is sufficient to establish that
\mathcal{H}
is indeed a Heegaard diagram for some link
J
in
\Sigma(L)
. We must now show that the link in question is
\widetilde{A}
, the lift of the annular axis. Recall that we reconstruct
J
by first connecting the
w
basepoints to the
z
basepoints in
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}
, and then connecting the
z
basepoints to the
w
basepoints in
\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
, undercrossing any arcs from the first step which we encounter. In particular, in each of these steps we may choose to draw the two arcs in question such that they are interchanged by the branched covering involution on
\Sigma_{\tau}
. This produces either a knot or a two-component link; in either case, projecting to
\mathbb{C}
produces a knot with a diagram on
\mathbb{C}
consisting of two arcs, one running from
\infty
to
0
and the second from
0
to
\infty
. The first of these arcs does not intersect the blue bridges
b_{i}
, and may be chosen to be the straight line segment shown in Figure
4.2
; the other does not intersect the red arcs
a_{i}
, and may be chosen to follow any path from
0
to
\infty
avoiding those arcs, undercrossing the vertical line segment as it passes it. This is the annular axis
A
. Its lift
\widetilde{A}
in
\Sigma(L)
is a knot if
\ell k(A,L)
is odd and is a two-component link if
\ell k(A,L)
is even. We therefore see that if
\ell k(A,L)
is odd we have

\displaystyle H_{*}(CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\backslash\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}}))

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFK}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L)\#(S^{1}\times S^{2}),\widetilde{A})

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFK}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L),\widetilde{A})\otimes H^{*}(S^{1})

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFK}}(\Sigma(mL),m\widetilde{A})\otimes V.

Here we again use the fact that
-\Sigma(L)=\Sigma(mL)
. Furthermore, note that
m\widetilde{A}
is the same as the lift of the annular axis
mA
of the annular link
mL
to its branched double cover
\Sigma(mL)
. If instead
\ell k(A,L)
is even, we have

\displaystyle H_{*}(CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\backslash\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}})

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFL}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L),\widetilde{A})

\displaystyle\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFL}}(\Sigma(mL),m\widetilde{A}).

We now recall from
[
Man06
, Proposition 7.1]
that the
anti-diagonal
divisor in
\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau})
is the set

\nabla=\left\{\{(u_{1},0,z_{1}),\dots,(u_{n},0,z_{n})\}:u_{i}^{2}+p_{(}z)=0,\ \exists\ i,j\text{ such that }z_{i}=z_{j}\text{ and }u_{i}=-u_{j}\right\}.

There is a filtration
\mathcal{F}
on the chain complex
CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\backslash\{w_{1},w_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}})
called the
anti-diagonal filtration
, coming from counting intersections of strips in the differential with
\nabla
. We denote the levels of this filtration by
\mathcal{F}_{i}
. There exists a combinatorial way to fix the absolute filtration level
i
; for discussion of this, see
[
Twe14
, Introduction and Section 4]
. Deleting the anti-diagonal divisor from the symmetric product and suitably perturbing the symplectic form to be convex at infinity returns the homology of the associated graded of the filtration in the usual way; that is, we have

H_{*}(CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\backslash\{w_{1},w_{2}\})\setminus\nabla,\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}}))\simeq\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}H_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{i}/\mathcal{F}_{i+1}).

Manolescu considers the involution induced by the map
(u,v,z)\mapsto(u,-v,z)
on the horizontal Hilbert scheme. When we need to distinguish this involution from others, we will refer to it as
\iota_{D}(u,v,z)=(u,-v,z)
. Here the choice of subscript is meant to remind the reader that this is the involution related to the branched
double
cover. He shows that we have fixed sets

((\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}))^{fix},\Sigma_{\alpha}^{fix},\Sigma_{\beta}^{fix})=(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2}\})\setminus\nabla,\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}}).

Seidel and Smith checked that this example admits a stable normal trivialization
[
SS10
, Lemma 31]
; their proof is reviewed in Section
7
. As a consequence
[
SS10
, Corollary 34]
, using their localization theorem for
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
Lagrangian Floer cohomology, recapped in this paper as Theorem
2.6
and Corollary
2.7
, they prove there is a spectral sequence

(4.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Kh}_{symp}(L)\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[\theta,\theta^{-1}]\rightrightarrows\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}H_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{i}/\mathcal{F}_{i+1})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[\theta,\theta^{-1}].

This is the symplectic analog of the Ozsváth-Szabó spectral sequence from Khovanov homology to Heegaard Floer homology. Combined with the spectral sequence from
H_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{i}/\mathcal{F}_{i+1})
to
\widehat{\mathit{HF}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L))\otimes H^{*}(S^{1})\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HF}}(\Sigma(mL))\otimes V
, this gives a dimension inequality

\dim(\operatorname{Kh}_{symp}(L))\geq 2\dim(\widehat{\mathit{HF}}(\Sigma(L))).

The anti-diagonal filtration and the spectral sequence it induces were further studied by Tweedy
[
Twe14
,
Twe15
]
, who showed among other results that the spectral sequence collapses on the
E_{1}
-page for two-bridge knots
[
Twe15
, Theorem 1.0.14]
; that is, for two-bridge knots we have

H_{*}(\mathcal{F}_{i}/\mathcal{F}_{i+1})\simeq\widehat{HF}(\Sigma(K)).

A conjecture of Seidel and Smith
[
SS10
, Section 4e]
, echoed by Tweedy
[
Twe15
, Conjecture 1.0.15]
, states that the spectral sequence in fact collapses immediately for every knot. As far as the authors are aware this remains open even for alternating knots.

We are now ready to prove Theorem
1.3
.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Let
\mathcal{F}_{i}
be the anti-diagonal filtration on
CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}-\{w_{1},w_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widetilde{a}},\mathbb{T}_{\widetilde{b}})
coming from counting intersections of pseudoholomorphic curves in the differential with
\nabla
, as above. Let

\nabla_{o}=\nabla\cap\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\}))

be the
annular anti-diagonal divisor
. The filtration
\mathcal{F}_{i}
induces a filtration on the Lagrangian Floer cochain complex
CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}})
. We denote this induced filtration by
\mathcal{G}_{i}
and refer to it as the
annular anti-diagonal filtration
. It is explicitly computed by counting intersections of strips in the differential with
\nabla_{o}
.

Now, the fixed set of the action induced by
\iota_{D}(u,v,z)=(u,-v,z)
on the horizontal Hilbert scheme
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*})=\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau})\setminus D_{o}
is precisely the complement of
\nabla_{o}
in
\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{0}\setminus\{w_{1},w_{2},z_{1},z_{2}\})
, and the fixed sets of the Lagrangians are unchanged from the non-annular case. This set of manifolds and Lagrangians inherits a stable normal trivialization from the non-annular case by restricting the vector bundle isomorphisms to the complement of
D_{o}\cap\nabla_{o}
. There is therefore by Corollary
2.7
a spectral sequence whose first page is

HF(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(A_{\tau}^{*}),\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta})\simeq\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(K)\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[\theta,\theta^{-1}]

and whose final page is isomorphic to

H_{*}(\mathcal{G}_{i}/\mathcal{G}_{i+1})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[\theta,\theta^{-1}].

There is then a spectral sequence from
\oplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}H_{*}(\mathcal{G}_{i}/\mathcal{G}_{i+1})
to
HF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\setminus\{w_{0},w_{1},z_{0},z_{1}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}})
. By the discussion above, this is isomorphic to either

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathit{HFK}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L),\widetilde{A})\otimes H^{*}(S^{1})\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFK}}(\Sigma(mL),m\widetilde{A})\otimes V

if
\ell k(L,A)
is odd and

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathit{HFL}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(L),\widetilde{A})\simeq\widehat{\mathit{HFL}}(\Sigma(mL),m\widetilde{A})

if
\ell k(L,A)
is even. The claimed rank inequalities follow.
∎

We now consider Corollary
1.4
. Let
K
be a two-bridge knot with its natural 2-periodic symmetry, and choose the annular axis
A
to be the axis of periodicity. Figure
5.1
is an example of this choice. We recall to the reader the following well-known lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

If
K
is two-bridge and
A
is its axis of periodicity, then the two lifts
\widetilde{K}
and
\widetilde{A}
are isotopic in the double-branched cover
\Sigma(K)
.

Proof.

If
K
is two-bridge and
A
is its axis of periodicity, then the quotient
\overline{K}
together with its axis
\overline{A}
is a link of two unknots, and
(S^{3},K\cup A)
is the branched double cover of
(S^{3},\overline{K}\cup\overline{A})
over
\overline{A}
. There exists an isotopy interchanging the components, for example by viewing
\overline{K}
as a one-bridge knot and arranging
\overline{A}
such that rotation around the origin in the plane of the page is the desired isotopy; see Figure
4.3
for an example. It is well-known that the double branched cover
\Sigma(K)
of
K
is the unique
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
cover of
\overline{K}\cup\overline{A}
; that is, the manifold
\Sigma(K)
may be constructed by taking the branched double cover over each of the components of
\overline{K}\cup\overline{A}
in either order (see, for example,
[
MV01
]
). In the diagram below,
(S^{3},B\cup K^{\prime})
denotes the branched double cover of
(S^{3},\overline{K},\overline{A})
over
\overline{K}
, with
B
being the unknot which is the lift of
\overline{K}
and
K^{\prime}
denoting the lift of
\overline{A}
. Since
\overline{A}
and
\overline{K}
are interchanged by isotopy in the bottom copy of the three-sphere,
B
is isotopic to a copy of the knot
K
in
(S^{3},B\cup K^{\prime})
.
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma(K),\widetilde{K}\cup\widetilde{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma(\overline{K})=S^{3},B\cup K^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma(\overline{A})=S^{3},K\cup A)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S^{3},\overline{K}\cup\overline{A})}

We see that the lift
\widetilde{A}
of
A
in
\Sigma(K)
is also up to isotopy the lift of
K
in its branched double cover
\Sigma(K)
. ∎

[formula]
[formula]

Figure 4.3
.
The quotient knot
\overline{K}
of the two-bridge link
K
of Figure
5.1
along its natural two-periodic symmetry, together with the image
\overline{A}
of the axis
A
under the quotient map. The knot is drawn in red and the axis in green. Note that the two components are interchanged by rotation.

We now complete the proof of Corollary
1.4
.

Proof of Corollary
1.4
.

This is the analog of
[
Twe15
, Theorem 1.0.14]
; indeed, Tweedy proves that for a two-bridge knot the differential on
CF(\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(\Sigma_{\tau}\backslash\{w_{1},w_{2}\}),\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}},\mathbb{T}_{\widehat{{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}})
preserves the filtration level of the anti-diagonal filtration, which therefore remains true after removing the basepoints
z_{0}
and
z_{1}
and their corresponding divisors in the symmetric product. However, in our setting we may additionally present a direct elementary proof. Consider a periodic two-bridge diagram for
K
, such as that for the trefoil in Figure
5.1
, and form a multi-pointed Heegaard diagram for
\Sigma(K)
by taking the branched cover over the four endpoints of the bridges as described above, à Figure
4.1
. We observe that this Heegaard diagram is necessarily
nice
in the sense of
[
SW10
,
MOS09
]
; a nice Heegaard diagram is one in which every region in the closure of the surface with the
\alpha
and
\beta
curves removed which does not contain a basepoint is either a square or a bigon. In the Heegaard diagram specified by taking the branched double cover of a two-bridge knot, every region which does not contain one of the four basepoints is in fact a rectangle. Sarkar and Wang showed that given a nice Heegaard diagram, all disks counted by the Heegaard Floer differentials have shadows which are embedded bigons or embedded rectangles on the Heegaard surface
[
SW10
]
. In our situation, this implies that every domain
D
which is the shadow of a curve
u
which counts for the differential is an embedded rectangle. In order for such a curve
u
to have positive intersection with
\nabla
, its shadow
D
must contain two points
(u,0,z)
and
(-u,0,z)
on the Heegaard diagram. This is not true of any embedded rectangle in the diagram, since the placement of the four basepoints ensures that all embedded rectangles occur either on the front half of the Heegaard diagram, facing the reader, or on the back half. We conclude that the anti-diagonal filtration is trivial for two-bridge knots. It follows that the spectral sequence from
H_{*}(\mathcal{G}_{i}/\mathcal{G}_{i+1})
to
\widehat{\mathit{HFK}}^{\raisebox{-3.0pt}{$\scriptstyle*$}}(\Sigma(K),\widetilde{K})\otimes H^{*}(S^{1})
collapses on the
E_{1}
page.
∎

5.
Spectral sequences for two-periodic links

In this section we prove Theorem
1.5
, establishing an analog of the Stoffregen-Zhang spectral sequence from the symplectic Khovanov homology of a doubly-periodic link to the annular symplectic Khovanov homology of the quotient link
[
SZ24
]
. As a warm-up, in Section
5.1
we start by proving a simpler spectral sequence from the annular symplectic Khovanov homology of a doubly-periodic link to the annular symplectic Khovanov homology of the quotient link, which is the analog of earlier work of Zhang
[
Zha18
, Theorem 1]
. We then proceed to the main result of the section in Section
5.2
.

Recall from Section
3.4
that
\tau_{Q,o}
denotes the configuration
\{-2n,\dots,-1,1,\dots,2n\}
in the set
\operatorname{Conf}^{4n,0}\cap\operatorname{Fix}(\iota)
, where
\iota
is the map
z\mapsto-z
on
\mathbb{C}
. Furthermore, recall that given a two-periodic link
L
, there is a canonical way of making
L
into an annular link by choosing the axis of periodicity as the annular axis. We may choose an annular bridge diagram for
L
which is compatible with the symmetry
\iota
and such that the endpoints of the bridges occur at the elements of
\tau_{Q,o}
, as in Figure
5.1
. Given such a bridge diagram, there is a quotient annular link diagram as in Figure
5.2
, such that the endpoints of the bridges occur on the points of the standard configuration
\tau_{o}=\{1,\dots,2n\}
.

5.1.
From the annular theory of a two-periodic knot to the annular theory of the quotient

[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]
[formula]

Figure 5.1
.
A 2-periodic bridge diagram for the trefoil knot. The intersection of the annular axis with the diagram is marked with a star.

Figure 5.2
.
The quotient of the periodic bridge diagram shown in Figure
5.1
. We continue to mark the annular axis with a star.

Consider the involution
\iota_{A}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muA^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}}\to A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}}
given by
(u,v,z)\mapsto(u,v,-z)
. As in Section
3.4
, this map induces an involution on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})
, which we here call
\iota_{A,H}
. Recall further that
Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}}
denotes the quotient of
A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}}
by
\iota_{A}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

The fixed point set of
\iota_{A,H}
is naturally isomorphic to
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}})
.

Proof.

Let
I
be a subscheme of points on
Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}}
which represents a point in
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}})
.
Let
\tilde{I}
be the pull-back of
I
under the quotient map
A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}}\to Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}}
.
The pull-back
\tilde{I}
is a subscheme of
A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}}
with length
2n
.
Since
\iota_{A}
is a lift of
\iota
to
A^{*}_{\tau}
, and in particular has no fixed points, the subscheme
\tilde{I}
necessarily lies in
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})\subseteq\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})
.
It is also clear that
\tilde{I}
is fixed by
\iota_{A,H}
. Therefore, the pullback map defines an embedding from
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}})
to the fixed set of
\iota_{A,H}
.

On the other hand, if
J
is a subscheme lying in the fixed set of
\iota_{A,H}
, then its image
\pi_{HC}(J)
in
\operatorname{Sym}^{2n}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})
must lie in the fixed set of the involution
\iota_{A,S}
on
\operatorname{Sym}^{2n}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})
induced by
\iota_{A}
.
Since
\iota_{A}
has no fixed points on
A_{\tau}^{*}
, it must be the case that
\pi_{HC}(J)=\{x,\iota_{A,S}(x)\}
for some
x\in\operatorname{Sym}^{n}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})
. Therefore,
J
is necessarily of the form
J_{x}\cap\iota_{A}^{*}J_{x}
for some length
n
subscheme
J_{x}\subset A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}}
such that
\pi_{HC}(J_{x})=x
.
But the image of the embedding from
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}})
precisely covers all subschemes of the form
J_{x}\cap\iota_{A}^{*}J_{x}
. We conclude that this embedding is an isomorphism.
∎

We may now use this identification to prove the promised first spectral sequence.

Proposition 5.2
.

There is a spectral sequence whose
E_{1}
page is isomorphic to

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[\theta,\theta^{-1}]

and whose
E_{\infty}
page is isomorphic to

\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(\overline{L})\otimes\mathbb{F}_{2}[\theta,\theta^{-1}].

It follows that

\dim\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)\geq\dim\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(\overline{L}).

Proof.

Given an annular bridge diagram for
L
a two periodic link, number the bridges in the diagram for
L
from right to left, as in the notation of Section
3.5
, so that
\iota(b_{i})=b_{2n+1-i}
, and choose similar notation for the undercrossing arcs
a_{i}
so that
\iota(a_{i})=a_{2n+1-i}
. Let
\overline{b}_{i}
and
\overline{a}_{i}
denote the quotient arcs in the bridge diagram.

Now, we recall that
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(L)
may be computed as the Lagrangian Floer cohomology of the triple
(\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}}),\Sigma_{\alpha},\Sigma_{\beta})
, where

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\alpha}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{a_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{a_{2n}}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\beta}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{b_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{b_{2n}}

are the products of the matching spheres lying above the arcs
a_{i}
and
b_{i}
as in (
3.3
) and Section
3.5
. The fixed sets of these two Lagrangians under the involution
\iota_{A,H}
are exactly the embedded copies of the products

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\bar{\alpha}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\bar{a}_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{\bar{a}_{n}}

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\bar{\beta}}

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{\bar{b}_{1}}\times\dots\times\Sigma_{\bar{b}_{2n}}

under the isomorphism of Lemma
5.1
. Therefore, the fixed sets of this triple under
\iota_{A,H}
are

((\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}}))^{fix},\Sigma_{\alpha}^{fix},\Sigma_{\beta}^{fix})\simeq(\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{A,\tau_{Q,o}}),\Sigma_{\bar{\alpha}},\Sigma_{\bar{\beta}}).

The righthand side computes
\operatorname{AKh}_{Q,A}(\overline{L})
, which we recall from Proposition
3.19
is isomorphic to
\operatorname{AKh}_{symp}(\overline{K})
. Moreover, it follows from the arguments of Section
7
that this setup admits a stable normal trivialization. We may now apply Corollary
2.7
to obtain the desired spectral sequence.
∎

Remark 5.3
.

The use of
\iota_{A}
in this section is for expositional simplicity only. It follows from Lemma
5.4
and Corollary
3.23
that on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A^{*}_{\tau_{Q,o}})
, the involutions
\iota_{A}
and
\iota_{B}
have isomorphic fixed sets, and each induce a spectral sequence from the annular symplectic Khovanov homology of a two-periodic link to the annular symplectic Khovanov homology of the quotient.

5.2.
From the ordinary theory of a two-periodic knot to the annular theory of the quotient

We now consider the involution
\iota_{B}\penalty\mskip 2.0mu\mathpunct{}\nonscript\mkern-3.0mu{:}\penalty\mskip 6.0mu plus 1.0muA_{\tau_{Q,o}}\to A_{\tau_{Q,o}}
given by
(u,v,z)\mapsto(-u,-v,-z)
, which induces an involution
\iota_{B,H}
on
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}})
. We maintain the same choices of notation for periodic annular bridge diagrams as in Section
5.1
. Recall from Section
3.4
that
Q_{B,\tau_{Q,o}}
denotes the quotient of
A_{\tau}^{*}
under
\iota_{B}
.

Lemma 5.4
.

The fixed point set of
\iota_{B,H}
is naturally isomorphic to
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{B,\tau_{Q,o}})
.

Proof.

Much as in the proof of Lemma
5.1
, one can construct an embedding from
\operatorname{Hilb}^{n,hor}(Q_{B,\tau_{Q,o}})
to
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}}^{*})\subseteq\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}})
.
Moreover, the image of this embedding is precisely the intersection between the fixed locus of
\iota_{B,H}
and
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}}^{*})
.
It therefore suffices to check that the fixed locus of
\iota_{B,H}
lies entirely in
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}}^{*})
.

Let
J\in\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}})
be an element in the fixed locus of
\iota_{B,H}
. If
y\in A_{\tau_{Q,o}}
lies in the support of
J
, then so does
\iota_{B}(y)
.
Notice that
\iota_{B}
has no fixed points in
A_{\tau_{Q,o}}
, so
y
and
\iota_{B}(y)
are two distinct points on the surface. Suppose for the sake of contradiction that
\pi_{A}(y)=0\in\mathbb{C}
. Then we also have
\pi_{A}(\iota_{B}(y))=0
.
Since
y
and
\iota_{B}(y)
are distinct points lying in
\pi_{A}^{-1}(0)
, the ideal
(\pi_{A})_{*}J
has length strictly less than
J
and therefore cannot be in
\operatorname{Hilb}^{2n,hor}(A_{\tau_{Q,o}})
